- Natural answer-aware conversations are pushed to `origin/feature/question-first-room` in commit `1080030`, 2026-10-04. Offline checks pass (104 Python tests with AI mocked, 115 React tests, production build). - **The question card now has materially more room, at 100% and at 110% zoom, 2026-10-01.** The user asked for more space for questions and citations and for a 110% browser zoom. Measuring first showed the card getting only 35% of a 1365x768 screen while 289px of question-plus-citation sat behind a 134px window. Four CSS-only changes in the base rules — stage gap 12 to 8px, stage padding 16/14 to 10/8, trimmed section headings, and the dock basis from `clamp(172px,22dvh,218px)` to `clamp(132px,19dvh,172px)`. The dock was the largest win and cost nothing: its content measures 135px, so ~37px of the old basis was dead space, and it still has a constant height so the stage never reflows between phases, which is why the user chose a fixed height originally. The answer box was not hidden. At 1365x768 the card grew 341 to 398px; at the 110% equivalent the card grew 273 to 344px and now shows the question and the whole three-line citation at once. The phone layout is byte-identical to before, verified at 932x430 and 800x640 where the dense media query re-declares every one of these properties and wins on source order. Offline verified: build passes, 115/115 React tests, 92/92 Python tests (AI mocked), no horizontal overflow, no console errors. Two measurement traps are recorded in the change entry, both of which nearly produced a confident false conclusion: injected stylesheets beat a later media query regardless of `!important`, and the browser served a stale cached CSS bundle that made the change look like a no-op. User visual review is pending.

- **A cited research line now arrives with its surrounding passage, 2026-10-01.** A PDF has no sentences, only visual lines, so a single cited line is often unreadable — the user hit a live citation that began without its subject and stopped dead on "Similarly,". The server now sends up to three lines either side of a cited line, and the room shows them dimmed above and below while the cited line itself is emphasized with weight, an accent left rule, and a recessed fill. The cited line is never altered, so "exact cited line" stays literally true and is still the only server-validated text in the block; the fidelity note now says which line was cited, and the text export labels the extras "Context before" and "Context after". Two deliberate limits: context never crosses a page boundary, and code citations get no context at all, because a code line is already complete and is shown with a line-number gutter where neighbours would blur the cited line. Offline verified: build passes, 115/115 React tests, 92/92 Python tests (AI mocked), and a browser check at 1440x900 measured the cited line at 12.86:1 and the dimmed context at 6.14:1, both passing AA, with the code path confirmed untouched and no console errors. Two tests caught a real bug during this work: an equality check on the composite page-and-line location label silently suppressed all context. User visual review is pending.

- **The question heading is pinned and the document page is centered, 2026-10-01.** Both defects were found by the user from live-room screenshots, not from preview mode. The card no longer scrolls as a whole: the panelist name and question number stay fixed at the top while the question, clarifications, previous answer, and citation scroll beneath them, so the asker stays identifiable even when a long question fills the card. The keyboard tab stop and focus ring moved with the scroll behavior to `.question-scroll`, since leaving them on the now non-scrolling section would have made the overflowing content unreachable by keyboard. Separately, `#page-citation` is no longer stretched to the full card width; it is `min(100%, 78ch)` and centered, which measures 787px and no longer frames a one-line citation in dead paper. Offline verified: build passes, 113/113 React tests, 88/88 Python tests (AI mocked). Pinning was proven by browser measurement, not by assertion: on `?preview=1&long=1` a 169px body scroll moved the question 169px and the heading 0px. Six preview states clean, no console errors, focus ring confirmed with a real Tab keypress, dense landscape intact. User visual review is pending.
- **The pypdf reading-order constraint is now written down, 2026-10-01.** The user reported that a cited line "wasn't on the PDF"; it was. `pypdf` reads a page's content stream, which is draw order rather than visual position, so in a slide deck a line numbered 16 on page 6 sat somewhere the user did not visually associate with it. The cited text is always genuinely in the upload, because `_ground_question` looks the excerpt up in the server's extracted lines and refuses the question when the lookup misses. `README.md` records this so a reviewer who hits the same surprise does not conclude the app hallucinated a citation.
- **Research citations now render as a document page, 2026-10-01.** A PDF or DOCX citation is presented as a page rather than as source code: filename, a raised page chip, and the cited passage on a lighter paper surface. Code citations are unchanged and still use the near-black source panel. The server extracts PDF text with `pypdf` and discards the original bytes, so this is a styled page built from extracted text, not a real PDF render, and a note under every passage says so plainly. A true render would need each PDF's bytes kept in room memory for the life of the room; that tradeoff was judged out of proportion for a prototype. Offline verified: build passes, 110/110 React tests (18 in `QuestionCard`, 12 in the new parser tests), 88/88 Python tests (AI mocked), and a live browser check at 1440x900 plus a 620px dense height confirmed the surface colour, chip treatment, sans stack, 3px focus ring, and no horizontal overflow on either the page or the card, with no page or console errors. No live AI defense and no hosted check. User visual review is pending.
- **`sample_project/` is restored.** It was found deleted in the working tree, which was the previously logged cause of 12 Python test failures on a fresh clone. It is now back from HEAD, `git ls-files` confirms both files are tracked, and the Python suite passes 88/88 without any fixture juggling.
- **Design decisions settled, 2026-10-01.** The user reviewed and chose to keep both open items as they stand: the muted indigo `--accent` (`#545a8c`) and the fixed-height lobby dock. Neither is treated as outstanding work. The accent carries the active-speaker, selected-tab, chosen-vote, and callout markers, which replaced the solid-black seat inversion that neumorphism disallows; the dock keeps a constant 172-218px so the stage never reflows between phases, and it is at its emptiest in the lobby, before any defense starts. If either is ever unwanted, `--accent` is a single token in `game/src/index.css` and the dock height is one `flex-basis` value.
- **Decision, 2026-10-01: full session persistence is deferred, not cancelled.** The user chose to keep the Download summary export and revisit stored history only if the app needs to scale. Do not start persistence work unprompted. When it does become necessary, three questions need answers first, because they are product and privacy decisions rather than engineering ones: who may see a teammate's answers, what can be deleted and by whom, and where the data lives. Cost drivers to weigh at that point: an in-process dict cannot survive a restart, Render's local disk is ephemeral so a file or SQLite store would be wiped on every deploy, an external free Postgres is the realistic minimum, and the one-worker one-instance rule would become stricter rather than looser. Expect roughly 2-4 days of careful work touching the room state machine, which is the highest-risk area. Only the transcript and coaching report should ever be stored; the uploaded source files, up to 600 KB per room, should stay ephemeral.
- A defense summary export is pushed to `origin/feature/question-first-room`. Once a room has at least one turn or a coaching report, the Transcript drawer offers a **Download summary** button that saves a plain-text copy of that defense in the browser: questions, each exact cited line and location, clarifications, team answers, factual timeout notes, the team coverage line, and the coaching report with its turn references. Nothing is uploaded or stored server-side, so the file is the team's only record once a room is gone. This is a deliberate smaller substitute for full session persistence and is not a replacement for it. Offline verified: 94/94 React tests including a test that reads the generated Blob back and asserts its real contents and file name; 88/88 Python tests (AI mocked); a browser check confirmed the button renders in the transcript and is correctly disabled in preview mode, where downloads are not accepted. An end-to-end click in a live room was not performed, because preview mode disables the action.

- Changed:
  - **Bug fix (reconnect):** `prevAnsweredRef` now initialises from the first snapshot's answered count (sentinel -1 → N on first arrival) so reconnecting mid-session no longer spuriously triggers the discussing animation. `handleUseRoom` and `handleLeaveRoom` also reset the baseline and cancel any in-flight discussing timer when entering or leaving a room.

  - **CSS bug fix (animation-delay shorthand conflict):** `.judge-waiting` previously used both an `animation:` shorthand (which implicitly sets delay to 0) and a separate `animation-delay:` override. Merged into a single shorthand with the delay embedded.

### 2026-09-30 — Neumorphic reskin of the room (CSS only)

- Session: root.
- Files: `game/src/index.css`, `game/tailwind.config.js`. No `.tsx`, no Python, no protocol change.
- Changed: the flat room's grayscale hairline look is replaced with a neumorphic / soft-UI language on a warm bone base (`--base #e9e7e2`, recessed `--base-deep #e1dfd9`). `--canvas` and `--paper` now resolve to the same base because soft UI requires a raised element to share its parent's background; only recessed wells go deeper. Hairline borders were replaced by one-light-source top-left shadow pairs, expressed as `--raise-sm` / `--raise-md` / `--press-sm` / `--press` tokens so the light source is consistent by construction. Borders became `1px solid transparent` instead of being removed, so box geometry is unchanged and no element shifts. The active panelist and defender seat now read as pressed-in with an indigo `--accent` marker and keep their state word; the drawer header left solid black; the dock became an extruded shelf; inputs, the chat well, the tab tracks, and the room-code card became inset wells. Primary actions keep a solid ink fill on purpose, since a neumorphic button cannot carry a strong background color. The amber urgent timer, the near-black citation panel, and the hard keyboard focus outline were deliberately left untouched as contrast anchors. `tailwind.config.js` had a dead navy/cyan `arena-*` palette that misdocumented the real colors; it now mirrors the bone tokens. The Tailwind pipeline itself was not touched, because its preflight is load-bearing for the hand-written rules.
- Verification: `npm run build` passed and `npm test` passed 84/84, confirming the change is CSS-only. Python suite: 88/88 pass, but only after restoring the `sample_project/` fixture — those files are deleted in the working tree by unrelated local changes, which causes 12 pre-existing `FileNotFoundError`s unrelated to this reskin; the fixture was restored, the suite run, and then the working tree was returned to its prior state. Offline, AI mocked; no live defense or paid call was made. Rendered the local preview rooms at `?preview=1`, `&vote=1`, `&complete=1` and in dense landscape, and confirmed via computed styles that raised, pressed, and inset surfaces all resolve with a consistent top-left light source, that `#source-block` still renders near-black, that there is no horizontal overflow, and that no page or console errors occur. Contrast was measured rather than assumed: the first `--subtle` value measured 3.76:1 on the bone base and failed WCAG AA, so it was darkened to `#67635c` (4.83:1). All text tokens now pass AA (ink 12.86, muted 6.20, subtle 4.83, accent 5.28 on base), and both shadow pairs stay soft at roughly 1.2–1.4:1 so they never read as hard edges.
- Remaining: the room has not been reviewed by the user on a normal desktop viewport — the headless browser in this session was clamped to a small viewport, so the only rendered evidence is dense landscape plus computed styles. The indigo `--accent` hue is new and awaits the user's approval. Also unchanged from earlier: `game/src/types.ts` remains a hand-maintained mirror of `Room.snapshot()` with no automated contract test. The dead `PhaserScene.tsx`, `ThreeDefenseScene.tsx`, `phaser/DefenseScene.ts`, and `JudgePanelOverlay.tsx` files and their tests still compile; the two `.tsx` files show as modified in the working tree from earlier unrelated local work and were not touched here.

### 2026-10-01 — Self-hosted Inter and deeper recessed wells

- Session: root.
- Files: `game/public/fonts/inter-latin.woff2` and `inter-latin-ext.woff2` (new), `game/src/index.css`, `game/tailwind.config.js`. No `.tsx`, no Python, no protocol change.
- Changed: the stylesheet had declared `Inter` first in the font stack for a long time but never loaded it — there was no `@font-face`, `@import`, or `<link>` — so every `Inter,` silently fell through to the system sans. Because `font-synthesis: none` was set and system sans fonts typically ship only 400 and 700, the weight ladder in use (`650`, `750`, `850`) was largely decorative: those declarations all collapsed onto the same rendered face. Inter is now self-hosted as a variable woff2 covering the 400-900 axis, split into latin and latin-ext unicode-range subsets, so the intermediate weights render as genuinely distinct steps. The fonts are bundled from `public/` rather than fetched from a CDN at runtime, so the app has no third-party font dependency and works offline. Recessed wells were also deepened from `#e1dfd9` to `#dcd9d2` so inputs, the chat log, and the vote well separate more clearly from the bone base, and `--subtle` was darkened to keep it legible on the darker surface.
- Verification: `npm run build` passed and `npm test` passed 84/84, confirming no behavior change. The built CSS contains both `@font-face` rules with the correct unicode ranges, and `dist/fonts/` contains both woff2 files, which are not gitignored. Verified in a browser that the font is genuinely loaded and the variable axis is live: canvas text measurement of identical copy at weights 400/650/750/850/900 produced five distinct rendered widths (329.45 / 338.20 / 342.05 / 346.46 / 348.83px), which is the direct evidence that the weight ladder is now real rather than synthesized. Confirmed the wells resolve to `rgb(220,217,210)` with inset shadows, the raised seat cards keep their outward pair, and there is no horizontal overflow. Contrast re-measured after every token change: on the base, ink 12.86:1, muted 6.20:1, subtle 5.46:1, accent 5.28:1; on the darker wells, ink 11.28:1, muted 5.43:1, accent 4.63:1, subtle 4.79:1. All text clears WCAG AA on both surfaces. Offline, AI mocked; no live defense or paid call was made.
- Remaining: the empty dock in the lobby still holds one centered sentence in a tall slab. That layout predates the reskin, and the raised rounded corners only made it more visible; shrinking the idle state or giving it content is a real change with a state-machine flavor, so it was deliberately left for a decision rather than done unasked. The indigo `--accent` still awaits an explicit brand decision. `game/src/types.ts` remains a hand-maintained mirror of `Room.snapshot()` with no automated contract test. The user has confirmed the reskin reads well on a desktop viewport but has not stated formal approval, so no review gate is marked here.

### 2026-10-01 — Neutral timeout copy and team coverage line

- Changed: two evidence-backed wording and framing improvements, both aimed at the rehearsal use case rather than generic engagement. First, the timeout notice in the transcript no longer reads "Time expired · no answer was submitted", which phrased a missed turn as an omission and matched none of the server's own discipline; it now reads "Time expired · question passed to the panel", which states the same fact without blaming the defender. It is deliberately not a score and reports coverage rather than answer quality, so it stays consistent with the coaching report's existing no-grade rule. ### 2026-10-01 — Research citations render as a document page, not as code

- Session: root.
- Files: `game/src/lib/citation.ts` (new), `game/src/lib/citation.test.ts` (new), `game/src/components/PageCitation.tsx` (new), `game/src/components/QuestionCard.tsx`, `game/src/components/QuestionCard.test.tsx`, `game/src/index.css`, `game/tailwind.config.js`, `README.md`, and this log. No Python, no protocol, no server change.
- Changed: `QuestionCard` now branches on the citation kind. A code citation is unchanged and still uses the near-black `#source-block`. A research citation renders through the new `PageCitation`: the filename, a page chip, and the cited passage on a lighter paper surface (`--page-paper: #f6f4ef`), with the page marker raised as a neumorphic chip to match the question number above it. The page marker is derived by the new `citation.ts` helpers, which parse the server's `Page {n} · extracted line {m}` string and degrade safely to a bare label when the format does not match, so a malformed location renders a document page rather than crashing or inventing a page number. The extracted passage is reproduced exactly, including its original leading and trailing whitespace, so the on-screen text still matches the uploaded file.
- **This is not a real PDF render, and the UI says so.** `research_files.py` extracts text with `pypdf` and discards the original bytes, so page layout, figures, tables, and equations are gone before the browser sees them. Rather than let the page framing imply a fidelity the app cannot deliver, a permanent note under the passage reads "Text extracted from the uploaded document. Figures, tables, and equations are not shown." A true render would require keeping each uploaded PDF's original bytes in room memory for the life of the room, which is out of proportion for this prototype. This is recorded as a known limitation, not a bug.
- Also fixed in this pass: `QuestionCard.tsx` still carried the old timeout copy that the room card and the transcript had already been made to disagree on, so the room card and the transcript contradicted each other about what happens when the answer clock runs out.
- Verification: `npm run build` passed and `npm test` passed 110/110, up from 94. Twelve new tests cover the parser, including page-only locations, the page-and-line form, a fallback label, a malformed string, and a missing value; six new `QuestionCard` tests assert that research citations render a document page and no code block, that the fidelity note is shown, that a DOCX label does not gain an invented page number, that code citations keep the monospace block, and that a malformed location still renders a page view. The Python suite passed 88/88 with AI mocked. In a live browser at 1440x900 on `?preview=1&research=1`, measured computed styles rather than assuming: the page surface is `rgb(246,244,239)`, the chip carries its intended background, padding, radius, and press shadow, the passage is set in the sans stack rather than the mono stack, the focus ring paints 3px, and neither the page nor the card overflows horizontally, at 900px and again at a 620px dense height where the chip and passage scale down cleanly. No page or console errors. Cross-checked `&long=1`, `&vote=1`, and `&review=1` to confirm code citations still use the code block. Offline and browser-verified, AI mocked; no live AI defense, no hosted check, and no physical second device were involved, and user visual review is still pending.
- Remaining: `sample_project/` was found deleted in the working tree and was restored from HEAD, which resolves the previously logged failure where a fresh clone failed 12 Python tests. Screenshot deletions under `screenshots/` and `screenshot/` are unrelated local state and were left untouched. The missing `main` landmark in `App.tsx`, the empty lobby dock, and the `types.ts` contract test remain open. Full session persistence stays deferred by decision.

### 2026-10-01 — Pinned question heading and centered document page

- Session: root.
- Files: `game/src/components/QuestionCard.tsx`, `game/src/components/QuestionCard.test.tsx`, `game/src/index.css`, `README.md`, and this log. No Python, no protocol, no server change, and no change to `PageCitation`, `citation.ts`, or any code-citation behavior.
- Changed: two layout defects, both found by the user from live-room screenshots rather than from preview mode. First, the question card was itself the scroll container, so once a long question and its citation exceeded the card height the panelist name and question number scrolled out of view while that panelist's seat still read "Asking"; a viewer watching the lower half of the screen could not tell who was asking or what had been asked. The card is now a flex column with `overflow: hidden`, the heading and review status are direct children above a new `.question-scroll` region, and only the question, clarifications, previous answer, and citation scroll. Second, the document page was stretched to the full 1074px card width, so a one-line citation sat at the far left of a wide band of empty paper and read as lopsided even though the box was mathematically centered; `#page-citation` is now `width: min(100%, 78ch)` and centered with `margin-inline: auto`, which measures 787px in the room and hugs short citations.
- The keyboard tab stop moved with the scroll behavior. `tabIndex={0}` and its focus ring were on the `<section>`, which no longer scrolls, so leaving them there would have made the overflowing content unreachable by keyboard. Both moved to `.question-scroll`, which carries an `aria-label` of "Question and cited source". The passage text stays left-aligned and ragged-right, `#source-block` stays full-width because long code lines genuinely need its horizontal scroll, and no page `min-height` was added because the card is already vertically tight.
- Verification: `npm run build` passed and `npm test` passed 113/113, up from 110. jsdom performs no layout, so three new tests assert the structure that produces pinning: the heading is not a descendant of `.question-scroll` while still being inside the card, both citation kinds render inside the scroll region, and the tab stop moved off the section. The Python suite passed 88/88 with AI mocked. Pinning was then proven by measurement in a browser rather than by assertion: on `?preview=1&long=1` at 1440x900, scrolling the body 169px moved the question text 169px and moved the heading 0px, and the card itself never overflowed. The document page measured 787px wide with its center exactly on the card's content-box center, a 0px delta. All six preview states showed the heading outside the scroll region with no card overflow and no page or console errors, the scrolled content clips at the scroll boundary rather than bleeding, dense landscape at 1440x620 keeps the flex layout, and a real Tab keypress paints a solid 3px `rgb(35,34,32)` focus ring on the scroll region. Offline and browser-verified, AI mocked; no live AI defense, no hosted check, and no physical second device were involved.
- Also recorded, after the user reported that a cited line "wasn't on the PDF" and it turned out to be: pypdf reads a page's content stream, which is draw order rather than visual position, so in the hackathon slide deck a line numbered 16 on page 6 belonged somewhere the user did not visually associate with it. The cited text is always genuinely in the upload, because `_ground_question` looks the excerpt up in the server's extracted lines and refuses the question when the lookup misses. `README.md` now states this so a reviewer who hits the same surprise is not left thinking the app hallucinated a citation.
- Remaining: visual review is the user's call and is not marked complete here. The lead-in still scrolls with the question, which is intentional because the question and its citation must read as one unit; promoting it into the pinned region is a one-line change if it proves load-bearing. A true PDF render remains unbuilt and would need a memory-budget decision plus a DOCX carve-out, since DOCX has no page boundaries and the page chip is structurally wrong for it. The missing `main` landmark, the empty lobby dock, and the `types.ts` contract test remain open. Full session persistence stays deferred by decision.

### 2026-10-01 — Research citations now show the cited line in its surrounding passage

- Session: root.
- Files: `question_generator.py`, `game_server.py`, `game/src/types.ts`, `game/src/components/PageCitation.tsx`, `game/src/components/QuestionCard.tsx`, `game/src/components/QuestionCard.test.tsx`, `game/src/lib/buildDefenseSummary.ts`, `game/src/previewState.ts`, `game/src/index.css`, `test_research_defense.py`, and this log.
- Changed: a PDF has no sentences, only visual lines, so a cited line on its own is frequently unreadable. The user hit this live: a citation read "Wi-Fi 6 module (2.4 GHz) for networking[12*L38-L41] [14*L19-L24]. Similarly," — no subject at the start, nothing after the trailing conjunction, and leftover markdown citation markers. That is not a rendering fault; `_lines` in `research_files.py` makes each visual line one citation unit. The server now serves up to three lines either side of a cited line as `evidence_before` and `evidence_after` on `GroundedQuestion`, carries them in the room snapshot and in the follow-up prompt, and the room renders them as dimmed context above and below the cited line, which is emphasized with a weight, an accent left rule, and a recessed fill. The fidelity note now also states that the cited line is the highlighted one, so context can never be mistaken for the citation. The plain-text export labels the extra lines "Context before" and "Context after" for the same reason.
- Two deliberate limits. Context is confined to the cited page, compared on the page portion of the location label rather than the whole label, because a PDF location encodes page and line together and a citation on page 2 must not display a line from page 1. And code citations get no context at all: a code line is already a complete logical unit and is shown with a line-number gutter, where neighbouring lines would blur which line was actually cited. The cited line itself is never altered, so "exact cited line" stays literally true and still server-validated.
- Verification: `npm run build` passed; `npm test` passed 115/115, up from 113; the Python suite passed 92/92, up from 88. Four new Python tests cover multi-line context, the page boundary, code citations receiving none, and the context reaching the room snapshot; a new `pdf_page_bytes` helper was added because the existing `pdf_bytes` puts each string on its own page and so could not express a multi-line page at all. Two new React tests cover the rendered emphasis and the no-context fallback. In a browser at 1440x900 on `?preview=1&research=1`, measured computed styles rather than assuming: the cited line is `rgb(35,34,32)` at weight 600 on a `rgb(233,231,226)` fill with a 3px accent rule, the context is `rgb(95,91,84)` at weight 400, and the two are clearly distinct. Contrast was measured rather than trusted, since `--subtle` has failed AA twice in this project: context on the page surface is 6.14:1, the cited line on its fill 12.86:1, and the accent rule 5.28:1, all passing. Dense landscape at 1440x620 scales the passage and the highlight together with no overflow, `?preview=1&long=1` confirms the code path still renders `#source-block` with no page view and no context, and there were no page or console errors. Offline and browser-verified, AI mocked; no live AI defense, no hosted check, no physical second device. User visual review still pending.
- A first attempt at the page-boundary check compared the whole location string, which made every PDF context line look like it came from another page and silently suppressed all context. Two tests caught it. The existing `pdf_bytes` helper was also masking the problem by placing one line per page. Both are recorded because the lesson generalizes: an equality check on a composite label needs the field it actually means.
- Remaining: context is limited to the same page and to three lines either side, so a paragraph whose sentence spans a page break still shows a fragment; widening it would mean sending a variable-length passage per turn. The `[12*L38-L41]` markers seen live are artifacts of whatever produced that PDF, not of this app, and cannot be cleaned without a real text-layer render. `session None` in that document's filename is likewise part of the uploaded name and is displayed verbatim; a server-side rejection of auto-generated-looking names was discussed but not built, and renaming at upload time is the current answer. Visual review is the user's call. The missing `main` landmark, the empty lobby dock, and the `types.ts` contract test remain open.

### 2026-10-01 — Reclaimed vertical space for the question card at 100% and 110% zoom

- Session: root.
- Files: `game/src/index.css` and this log. No component, no Python, no protocol change, and no change to any citation behavior.
- Changed: the user asked for more room for questions and citations, and separately for 110% browser zoom on a 1365x768 window. Measuring the room showed the question card receiving only 35% of the screen while 289px of question-plus-citation sat behind a 134px window, so the citation was being cut off during a real defense. Four changes, all in the base rules: stage row gap 12px to 8px, stage vertical padding 16/14px to 10/8px, section heading margin and size trimmed, and the dock basis changed from `clamp(172px, 22dvh, 218px)` to `clamp(132px, 19dvh, 172px)`. The dock was the single largest win and cost nothing visually: its content measures 135px, so roughly 37px of the old 172px basis was dead space, and the dock keeps a constant height so the stage still never reflows between phases, which was the reason the user chose a fixed height originally. The answer box was not hidden or removed.
- Verification: `npm run build` passed, `npm test` passed 115/115 unchanged, and the Python suite passed 92/92 with AI mocked; this is CSS-only, so no test asserts any of it. Everything below is browser measurement. At 1365x768 the card grew from 341px to 398px and the scroll window from 243px to 300px, cutting hidden content from 180px to 123px. At the 110% zoom equivalent, a CSS viewport of 1241x698, the card grew from 273px to 344px and the window from 181px to 252px, cutting hidden content from 231px to 160px, and the 110% view now shows the question and the entire three-line citation at once. The phone layout is unchanged and this was verified rather than assumed: the dense-landscape media query sits after these base rules and re-declares every one of them, and at both 932x430 and 800x640 the computed gap, dock basis, dock height, heading size, and card height are identical to the pre-change baseline, with the dock still not clipping the answer box. No horizontal overflow and no page errors at either zoom.
- A measurement mistake worth recording, because it nearly produced a false conclusion. Verifying the phone was unaffected with `page.addStyleTag` gave the wrong answer twice: injected stylesheets are appended to the end of the document, so they beat the dense media query on source order whether or not `!important` is used. The first attempt appeared to show the phone card shrinking from 178px to 132px, which would have meant the change was harmful. The correct method is to edit the real base rules and let the cascade resolve, which is what the shipped change does. A second, smaller trap: the running browser served a stale cached CSS bundle and reported the old gap and dock values, so a first pass concluded the change had no effect at all. `Network.setCacheDisabled` plus a cache-busting query fixed it. Neither the code nor the conclusion was wrong, but both would have been reported as confident nonsense without checking what the browser was actually loading.
- Remaining: the citation still needs scrolling at 110% for a question longer than the mock one, which is correct behavior, and 160px of overflow remains in that worst case. Two further levers were considered and not taken: collapsing the dock to its tabs when the viewer cannot answer, worth about 90px but causing the stage to reflow between phases, which the user previously chose to avoid; and compacting the eight seat cards, worth about 50px but cramping four names per row. The user prefers the 110% view, so the proportional-budget option, expressing the seat rows and dock in viewport units so the card holds its share as zoom changes, remains available and is the more principled fix. Visual review is the user's call and is not marked complete here.

### 2026-10-09 — Three availability rescan findings fixed locally

- Session: root `availability-security-fixes`, closed by this entry. User authorized fixing the completed Standard rescan's three medium availability findings. The canceled deep scan remains canceled; no new scan, live-provider call or production stress was performed.
- Anonymous join requests now count actual streamed bytes before FastAPI JSON decoding, including unknown rooms, absent/false Content-Length and chunked bodies. Admission is bounded to 4 KB, 10 seconds, 32 global and eight per client network with network rate limiting and cancellation/disconnect cleanup. Legitimate UTF-8 names, lowercase room codes and normal joins remain supported.
- Guest sockets reserve global/network capacity before hello, enforce two tabs per player/eight per room, retain player/network connection and message budgets across reconnects, reject binary actions and suppress duplicate-vote publications. Snapshots serialize once per recipient player, coalesce revision publications and bound concurrent sends; slow recipients are removed/reassigned without blocking healthy peers. Error sends and room-removal closure are also deadline-bounded. Last-tab disconnect retains existing online/reassignment rules.
- DOCX package parsing, including central-directory reads and every related member, now occurs in a credential-free Linux child. Limits: 128 MiB address space, 10 CPU seconds, 20 wall seconds, 512 members, 8 MB per member, 32 MB total expanded bytes and 1000:1 expansion ratio. Duplicate/encrypted/oversized parts are rejected before Document loading. Timeouts and canceled upload requests kill and reap the worker before releasing the parser slot; exact paragraph/table citation order remains. Non-Linux servers fail closed for DOCX with a TXT/Markdown alternative; PDF handling is unchanged.
- Files for this fix: game_server.py, resource_limits.py, research_files.py, new document_parser_worker.py and test_availability.py, requirements.txt, run_local.sh, render.yaml, README.md, test_research_defense.py, test_research_coverage.py and PROJECT_LOG.md. Existing uncommitted account/payment/React work, private helpers, screenshots and unrelated deletions remain preserved.
- Review: fresh read-only pre-patch investigator and one fresh read-only bypass/regression reviewer under the Fix Finding skill. Parent confirmed and fixed the missing manual Render flags and the installed Uvicorn auto/sansio backend's ignored queue bound. Launch commands now explicitly select the declared websockets backend. A small offline actual-protocol fixture confirms four completed messages queue and further reads wait; frame limit is 20 KB. No remaining concrete join/DOCX or socket lifecycle issue reported by that review.
